115 年 9 月海關進出口貿易初步統計 
一、進出口貿易變動 
（一）出口 
9 月出口 872.2 億美元，創歷年單月新高，較上月增 5.9%，較上年
同月增 60.9%(按新臺幣計算增 68.2%)；第3 季出口 2,449.2 億美元，
較上年同季增 44.6%；累計 1 至 9 月 6,615.5 億美元，較上年同期增
46.2%(按新臺幣計算增 49.1%)。 
（二）進口 
9 月進口 635.9 億美元，亦創歷年單月新高，較上月增 5.8%，較上
年同月增 51.7%(按新臺幣計算增 58.6%)；第 3 季進口 1,818.0 億美
元，較上年同季增 44.3%；累計 1 至 9 月 5,010.0 億美元，較上年同
期增 41.3%(按新臺幣計算增 43.8%)。 
（三）出入超 
9 月出超 236.3 億美元，為單月新高，較上年同月增 113.4 億美元；
累計 1 至 9 月出超 1,605.5 億美元，較上年同期增 625.4 億美元。

2 
二、出口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
由於創新科技應用商機持續擴展，推升相關供應鏈出貨動能，加上
消費性電子新品備貨需求與產品價格上漲效應之挹注， 9 月出口較
上年同月增 330.0 億美元(+60.9%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 資通與視聽產品出口 391.3 億美元(歷年單月次高)， 較上年同月增
198.6 億美元(+1.0 倍)， 其中電腦及其附屬單元增157.4 億美元(+1.1
倍)、儲存媒體增 17.6 億美元(+2.2 倍)、交換器及路由器增 10.5
億美元(+1.1 倍)。 
2. 電子零組件出口 311.3 億美元(歷年單月次高)， 較上年同月增97.1
億美元(+45.3%)，其中積體電路增 93.9 億美元(+46.1%)、印刷電
路增 1.4 億美元(+33.7%)、 電容器及電阻器增0.8 億美元(+32.0%)。 
3. 礦產品出口 22.6 億美元，較上年同月增 10.5 億美元(+86.4%)，其
中石油煉製品增 10.1 億美元(+99.0%)。 
4. 機械出口 27.4 億美元(歷年單月次高)，較上年同月增 6.4 億美元
(+30.7%)，其中生產半導體等機械增 2.7 億美元(+61.0%)、未列名
具特殊功能機械增 0.6 億美元(+73.6%)。 
5. 基本金屬及其製品出口 26.9 億美元，較上年同月增 5.0 億美元
(+22.9%)，其中銅及其製品增 2.5 億美元(+65.1%)、鋼鐵及其製品
增 1.4 億美元(+11.7%)。

1. 第 3 季出口較上年同季增 755.9 億美元(+44.6%)，以資通與視聽產
品增 367.6 億美元(+55.0%)、電子零組件增 308.4 億美元(+51.2%)
增加較多。 
2. 累計 1 至 9 月出口較上年同期增 2,089.8 億美元(+46.2%)，其中資
通與視聽產品增 1,222.2 億美元(+72.4%)、電子零組件增 726.6 億
美元(+45.7%)。

等出現結構性轉變，爰另予併計。

三、進口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
隨 AI 產業鏈全球分工與出口引申需求擴增，電子零組件、資通產品
及半導體設備進口維持強勁，加上國際農工原料行情處於高檔，9
月進口較上年同月增 216.7 億美元(+51.7%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 農工原料進口440.5 億美元， 較上年同月增163.9億美元(+59.3%)，
其中電子零組件增 112.6 億美元(+82.5%)、電腦之零附件增 19.4
億美元(+1.5 倍)、礦產品增 17.1 億美元(+38.9%)。 
2. 資本設備進口 128.0 億美元， 較上年同月增 36.0 億美元(+39.1%)，
其中資通設備增 18.4 億美元(+75.0%)、半導體設備增 12.4 億美元
(+34.9%)，交通運輸設備減 1.4 億美元(-46.4%)。 
3. 消費品進口 62.1 億美元，較上年同月增 16.5 億美元(+36.0%)，其
中固態非揮發性儲存裝置增 16.4 億美元(+5.3 倍)、筆電增 0.5 億
美元(+43.8%)，藥粧用品減 1.2 億美元(-16.6%)。

四、主要出口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
9 月對東協出口創新高，對美國、陸港為單月次高 
1. 對美國出口 274.6 億美元，較上年同月增 141.5 億美元(+1.1 倍)，
其中資通與視聽產品增 124.1 億美元(+1.3 倍)、 電子零組件增10.1
億美元(+1.3 倍)、機械增 2.2 億美元(+42.3%)。 
2. 對中國大陸與香港出口 206.2 億美元，較上年同月增 47.8 億美元
(+30.2%)，其中電子零組件增 34.9 億美元(+30.5%)、資通與視聽
產品增 5.7 億美元(+39.0%)、機械增 1.9 億美元(+43.7%)。 
3. 對東協出口 188.8 億美元，較上年同月增 91.2 億美元(+93.4%)，
其中資通與視聽產品增 49.7 億美元(+2.2 倍)、電子零組件增 32.0
億美元(+63.3%)、礦產品增 4.8 億美元(+1.4 倍)。 
4. 對歐洲出口 45.3 億美元，較上年同月增 7.7 億美元(+20.6%)，其
中資通與視聽產品增 5.7 億美元(+34.1%)、電子零組件增 2.2 億美
元(+56.6%)，基本金屬及其製品減 0.8 億美元(-20.5%)。 
5. 對日本出口 30.7 億美元，較上年同月增 3.6 億美元(+13.4%)，其
中電子零組件增 1.5 億美元(+11.0%)、基本金屬及其製品增 0.6 億
美元(+32.5%)、機械增 0.5 億美元(+29.1%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 9 月對美國出口 2,077.8 億美元，較上年同期增 773.1 億美
元(+59.3%)，對中國大陸與香港出口 1,600.9 億美元，增 355.3 億美
元(+28.5%)， 對東協出口1,339.6 億美元 ， 增463.6 億美元(+52.9%)，
對歐洲出口 421.8 億美元 ， 增138.4 億美元(+48.8%)， 對日本出口277.7
億美元，增 58.3 億美元(+26.6%)。

五、主要進口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
9 月自中國大陸與香港進口 135.0 億美元 ， 較上年同月增50.1 億美元
(+59.1%)，自南韓進口 116.3 億美元，增52.6 億美元(+82.5%)，自東
協進口 106.0 億美元，增 52.6 億美元(+98.4%)，自日本進口 64.3 億
美元，增 17.7 億美元(+38.0%)，自歐洲進口 50.2 億美元，增 3.5 億
美元(+7.4%)，自美國進口 48.1 億美元，增 6.5 億美元(+15.5%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 9 月自主要國家/地區進口與上年同期比較 ， 自東協增77.6%、
自南韓增 65.8%、自中國大陸與香港增 42.8%、自日本增 25.7%、自
美國增 21.4%、自歐洲增 10.8%。

六、主要國家/地區貿易概況 
（一）主要國家/地區今年 1-8 月外貿景氣普遍走升，以南韓(1-9 月)出口年
增 56.8%最為強勁，香港、新加坡亦分別年增 49.0%、39.8%；中國
大陸、美國出口分別年增 19.3%及 14.6%，德國(1-7 月)、日本各增
9.7%、8.3%。 
（二）主要國家/地區進口同步上揚，累計 1-8 月以香港進口年增 50.5%較
突出，新加坡、中國大陸分別年增 37.3%、27.0%，南韓(1-9 月)、德
國(1-7 月)分別增 19.7%、10.0%，日本、美國各增 7.8%、4.2%。

### Page 7

7
圖 5 2026 年主要國家/地區出進口年增率
七、綜合分析
（一）受惠於人工智慧(AI)、高效能運算及雲端服務等應用商機持續擴展，
加以步入傳統外銷旺季 、產品價格上漲效應，9 月出口 872.2 億美元，
年增 60.9%，連續 35 個月正成長；進口635.9 億美元，年增51.7%，
主因 AI 產業鏈國際分工與出口引申需求擴張，及國際原物料行情居
高；出、進口值及出超 236.3 億美元齊創單月新高。累計 1 至 9 月出
口年增 46.2%、進口增 41.3%。
（二）9 月主要貨品出口普遍較上年同月成長，以資通與視聽產品增 1.0 倍
最佳，包括顯示卡、伺服器、儲存媒體、 交換器及路由器等出口均
呈倍數增加 ；其次為礦產品增 86.4%，主因全球油品供應偏緊支撐價
格維持高檔；電子零組件隨 AI 應用及消費性電子新品備貨需求升溫，
增 45.3%，機械、電機產品亦各增 3 成上下。累計 1 至 9 月資通與視
聽產品、電子零組件出口併計增 59.5%，占總出口比重 79.0%。

（三）主要市場方面，9 月對美國、東協出口值分創歷年單月次高、新高，
年增率各為 1.1 倍及 93.4%，合計對總出口增加貢獻達 7 成；對中國
大陸與香港、歐洲、日本出口各年增 30.2%、20.6%、13.4%。累計 1
至 9 月對五大市場出口值皆為歷年同期最高，其中對美國、東協均
提前刷新全年紀錄 ， 分別年增59.3%、52.9%，對歐洲出口年增48.8%，
對陸港、日本各增 28.5%、26.6%。
（四）展望未來，全球貿易政策及地緣政治情勢變化等，仍增添總體 經濟
不確定性，惟隨人工智慧應用 持續深化，國際雲端服務業者 及各國
加速 AI 基礎建設投資，帶動半導體、伺服器等相關供應鏈需求擴增，
加以科技新品備貨、中國大陸及歐美年底採購旺季將屆， 均有利挹
注我國外銷動能，第 4 季出口可望延續成長態勢。

増加が、他産業の生産や建設活動を圧迫する兆候も

耐久財製造業は、軍需やデータセンター（DC）増
設の旺盛な需要が、他の製品の生産を圧迫して
いると報告ベージュブック
（2026/10） テクノロジー企業がDC向け電気設備の買い占め
を試みたが、供給業者は他案件への供給を優先
し、これを拒否

米国：AI関連を中心に製造業生産は堅調も、資材・設備の需給ひっ迫が他産業に波及
 米国の製造業生産は、AI関連投資を背景にコンピューター・電子製品、一次金属、一般機械などが堅調
― 一方、非耐久財や家具など消費関連の生産は軟調に推移し、業種間のばらつきが拡大
 FRBは、データセンター建設に伴う鋼材・電気設備などの需要増加が、他の建設案件や生産活動に影響していると指摘
―AI投資の拡大が関連産業の生産を押し上げる一方、供給制約を通じて他産業の活動を圧迫する兆候も
― 当面はAI投資による成長押し上げ効果が期待される一方、供給制約や価格上昇を通じた負の影響にも注意が必要

日本：全国コアCPIは再び2％超え。基調的なサービス価格も伸び高まる
5
 9月の全国コアCPIは前年比＋2.1％（前月：同＋1.7％）と加速。2025年12月以来の2％超に
― 原油高・円安等のコスト上昇もあり、家事用消耗品や教養娯楽用品等を中心に伸び率が拡大
― 一般サービス価格も伸び加速。シルバーウィークの並びによる宿泊料や外食費の上昇などが主因
 特殊要因を除いた一般サービス価格もじわり上昇
― 変動の大きい宿泊費や私立高校無償化による押し下げを除いた基調的な一般サービス価格に上昇圧力。人件費増の
価格転嫁が進んでいる可能性を示唆

### Page 7

日本：連合は2027年春闘で賃上げ要求を強化。高水準の賃上げ持続と物価への波及が焦点
6
 連合は、2027年春闘賃上げ要求の目安を「6％中心、5％以上」と発表
―5％以上の賃上げを最低目標として明確化。実質賃金1％上昇の定着をめざす方針
― 中小企業は大企業との格差是正に向け「6％以上」が目標。過去4年のインフレで実質賃金が低下した分の回復も要求
― また、新たに、2030年に2026年の賃金水準を15％程度上回る中期目標を設定。持続的な賃金上昇をめざす
 賃上げ余力は企業規模・業種で濃淡。中小企業等で高い「賃上げ5％の壁」
― なお、6％に近い賃上げが実現する場合、サービス価格への波及が強まり、基調的物価上昇率が2％を上回る可能性

### Page 8

欧州：2027年度予算審議が本格化。生活苦によるデモが拡大する中、財政赤字は高水準
7
 2027年度予算審議が本格化。財政赤字は総じて高止まり
― 一定の防衛費・エネルギー関連支出を柔軟化する特例措置の適用後も、複数の国でEU財政基準で定める赤字幅を超
過。スペインは予算案を巡って議会解散。フランス・ベルギーも10/15時点でEUに予算案を未提出
 生活改善を求める抗議活動が欧州各国で発生。支出圧力の高まりは財政再建の障壁に
― フランスの学生によるデモを発端に、生活費の高騰や公共サービスの劣化に対する抗議活動は欧州各国に拡大。一部
は暴動に発展し、警察官と衝突する事態に
9～10月に発生した主な抗議活動主要国の財政収支実績と見通し
（出所） 各種報道より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
ベルギー

2026年・2027年 みずほ世界経済見通し
～転換点は近いのか、投資主導経済の行方を探る～
（2026年10月2日発刊）
調査部レポートのご紹介 PR
2026年10月作成
レポート
URL
レポート
二次元
コード
世界経済はどこへ向かうのか？中東危機やAI・レジリエンス関連の投資が
もたらす変化や、経済・金融市場の行方を展望
【ポイント①】
中東危機が⾧期化するなかでも、AI・レジリエンス関連の旺盛な投資需要に
支えられ、世界経済は3％台の成⾧ペースを維持
【ポイント②】
世界はインフレ・高金利の新常態へ。成⾧・負担の偏在が経済主体間の
「耐性格差」を拡大
【ポイント③】
海外主要中銀はインフレ警戒から利上げモードへ、日銀も物価上振れを警戒

MSCI 降評風險升高，印尼股市保守看待
2026 年 10 月 27 日
一、 市場動態
1. MSCI 擬將印尼降至獨立市場，降評風險明顯升高：MSCI 於 10 月 15 日宣布，啟動
將印尼股市由「新興市場（Emerging Market）」直接降至「獨立市場（Standalone
Market） 」的諮詢程序，意見徵詢將於2027 年 4 月 30 日截止，最晚於 2027 年 6 月
30 日公布最終決定 。MSCI 原先於今年 1 月提出可能降至 「邊境市場 （Frontier Market
） 」 ，此次則跳過「邊境市場」提出直接將該國股市降至「獨立市場」的方案，顯示對

印尼市場治理的疑慮進一步加深。雖然「邊境市場」仍可能是替代分類方案，但MSCI
強調，除非有明確證據顯示市場治理及運作出現實質改善，否則預期將依目前提案重
新分類，顯示此次降評風險較過去顯著升高。
2. 股權透明度與交易秩序問題為 MSCI 主要疑慮：MSCI 指出，印尼股市長期存在股權
結構不透明、實際自由流通股比例難以確認、疑似協調交易，以及市場監理執法成效
不足等問題，影響國際投資人對市場公平性與可投資性的信心。雖然印尼主管機關已
提出強化持股超過 1%股東資訊揭露、建立高持股集中度（HSC）監理機制，以及規
劃逐步將最低自由流通股比例提高至 15%等改革措施，但MSCI 認為相關改革尚未展
現持續且具實質性的改善成果。由於問題涉及市場制度與治理結構，改善所需時間可
能較一般短期市場波動更長。
3. 股市反彈後再度回落，外資撤離壓力仍高：受降評消息衝擊，印尼雅加達綜合指數 10
月 16 日重挫 11.57%，創 1998 年以來最大單日跌幅，19 日再跌 3.64%，盤中最低
觸及 4,907.37 點 。 雖然20 日至 23 日連續四個交易日反彈 ， 但26 日再度下跌 1.22%

，收於 5,373.09 點，各產業類股全面收黑，顯示市場信心仍不穩定。根據 Reuters
報導，截至 10 月 19 日，2026 年外資已累計淨賣超印尼股票約 48.8 億美元。此外，
印尼證交所 26 日宣布，將引進獨立專家及外國投資人代表參與高持股集中度審查，
並自 11 月起加強調查疑似協調交易，必要時凍結可疑帳戶，以回應 MSCI 對市場制
度的疑慮。
二、 下跌原因分析：
1. 股市：潛在指數剔除與資金外流，持續壓抑市場評價
若印尼股市最終降至「獨立市場」， 原則上將不再納入MSCI 新興市場及邊境市場相關
指數，追蹤相關指數的被動式基金將須依規則調整持股，實際處理方式仍以 MSCI 後
續公告為準。若改降至「邊境市場」，則可能獲得部分邊境市場資金承接，衝擊相對較
小。Reuters 引述多家金融機構估計，印尼股市降至獨立市場可能引發約 30 億至 60

### Page 2

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
億美元被動資金流出，其中 UBS 估計約 40 億美元，並指出主動型基金另有約 150 億
美元潛在撤資風險，惟實際規模仍視最終分類結果及基金策略而定。
2. 匯率：外資撤離增加印尼盾貶值壓力
印尼股市的降評風險不僅衝擊股市，也可能透過外資撤離增加印尼盾貶值壓力。印尼
盾 10 月 16 日盤中一度貶至 18,070 兌 1 美元，為 7 月下旬以來最弱水準之一，當日
收盤約 18,000。若後續國際資金持續流出，可能進一步增加股匯市波動，對以外幣計
價、且未完全避險的印尼基金及 ETF 形成額外壓力。雖然印尼央行可透過匯市干預及

流動性管理措施穩定金融市場，但在資金外流壓力尚未緩解前，印尼盾仍可能維持偏
弱格局。
3. 基金與 ETF：指數權重下滑，單一市場曝險風險升高
MSCI 相關指數調整已對印尼股票產生實質影響。Reuters 引用 LSEG 資料顯示，印尼
在 MSCI 新興市場指數的權重已由 2026 年初的 1.16%降至 9 月底的 0.43%，相關成
分股亦由年初 18 檔減至 9 檔，反映部分指數資金調整壓力已提前出現。對印尼單一
國家基金及 ETF 而言，主要風險來自股市下跌、資金流出及印尼盾波動；主動式基金
雖可透過選股降低部分影響，但仍難以完全迴避整體市場風險。不同產品實際受到的
影響，仍應依追蹤指數、主要持股及匯率避險安排個別評估。
三、 市場看法：
1. 制度性問題難以速解，降評風險將持續牽動市場：我們認為，此次 MSCI 降評風險並
非單純反映經濟成長放緩或企業獲利不佳，而是涉及市場透明度、交易公平性與監理
執法等結構性問題。尤其 MSCI 不僅啟動降評諮詢，更持續限制印尼股票的部分指數

調整，包括不提高外資納入因子、不新增相關成分股，以及不進行向上規模分類調整
，顯示市場可投資性疑慮尚未獲得有效解決。儘管政府試圖推動改革，但制度修正仍
須經過執行與成效驗證，才能重新取得國際投資人的信任，因此短期內降評風險仍難
完全消除。
2. 外資撤離與指數權重下降，市場信心修復恐需較長時間：印尼今年以來外資已大幅賣
超，MSCI 新興市場指數中的權重亦明顯下降，顯示國際投資人對當地市場的疑慮已
逐漸反映於實際資金流向。我們認為，即使印尼最終成功避免降評，部分國際機構投
資人仍可能因市場透明度、流動性及治理品質疑慮而維持較低曝險。此外，MSCI 對
部分指數調整的凍結措施尚未解除，短期內新增指數資金流入機會仍受到限制，預期
國際資金回流與市場評價修復均可能需要較長時間。
3. 監理改革力度加大，但尚不足以扭轉保守展望：印尼政府近期持續加強改革，包括提
高資訊透明度、強化交易監控、對違規交易開罰，以及引進外部獨立人士參與監理審
查。10 月 26 日該國政府進一步提出主動調查疑似協調交易及凍結可疑帳戶等措施，

11 月 2 日起亦規劃將高持股集中度股票排除於雅加達綜合指數之外 。 相關改革有助改

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
善交易秩序及短期市場信心，但部分措施仍待正式實施，且實際成效尚未獲得 MSCI
認可。考量制度性問題改善需要時間，政策利多雖可能帶動短線反彈，但尚不足以改
變對印尼股市中期前景的保守判斷。
4. 股市跌深不代表利空出盡，中期風險報酬吸引力偏低：印尼股市在 10 月 16 日及 19
日大幅修正後，雖曾連續四個交易日反彈，但 26 日再度回落，顯示市場尚未形成穩
定回升趨勢。我們認為，當前股市面臨的不僅是短期賣壓，更包括國際投資人信任下
降及風險溢價提高，即使企業基本面未同步惡化，仍可能因評價下修而承受股價壓力

。因此，短線不排除政策利多或跌深後出現技術性反彈，但在 MSCI 態度、外資流向
與印尼盾走勢明顯改善前，反彈延續性仍有疑慮，印尼股市中期展望維持保守。
四、市場展望：
1. 印尼股市維持保守展望，單一市場風險報酬吸引力不足：考量 MSCI 降評風險尚未解除、
外資撤離壓力持續 ， 以及市場治理改革仍有待驗證 ， 我們認為印尼股市未來數季的風險報
酬吸引力相對有限。即使近期股價明顯回落，仍難認定相關風險已充分反映。相較市場制
度成熟、國際資金流動性較佳的新興市場，印尼股市可能持續面臨較高風險溢價，因此對
單一印尼市場的集中曝險應維持審慎態度。
2. 此事件對新興市場指數與東協指數影響相對有限：根據 MSCI 資料，截至2026 年 9 月底
，印尼股市在 MSCI Emerging Market 指數與 MSCI AC ASEAN 指數中的權重分別約為
0.43%與 7.55%，因此我們認為印尼潛在降評對相關指數的直接衝擊相對有限。此外，新
興市場各國經濟結構與產業組成有所差異 ， 區域型基金可透過跨國分散降低單一市場風險

。惟後續仍須留意印尼事件對整體新興市場投資信心造成的外溢影響 ， 以及全球貿易與匯
率波動風險。

美 
國 
PMI 大幅走升、新屋銷售回穩，消費信心薄弱，美中關係暫現緩和訊號 
經濟 
⚫ S&P Global 公布，9 月美國採購經理人綜合指數(PMI Composite Output 
Index)初值由上月的 56.0 升至 58.4，創逾 5 年新高；服務業及製造業
PMI 分別升至 58.7 及 57.0。在內需強勁帶動下，新訂單與就業同步成
長，惟供應鏈瓶頸及能源成本攀升推升通膨壓力。 
⚫ 8 月美國新屋銷售年化率為 68.4 萬戶，月增 6.4%、年減 2.0%，顯示銷
售較前月回升但需求尚未完全恢復。中西部與南部為主要支撐，惟房價
年減、庫存仍維持 48 萬戶以上，供給相對充裕，後續銷售能否延續仍
待觀察。 
⚫ 9 月美國消費者信心指數降至 48.1，月減 7.0%、年減 12.7%，創近四個
月低點。當前狀況與預期指數同步下滑，反映高物價、燃料價格與貿易
不確定性持續壓抑信心；同時通膨預期升溫，後續消費動能仍待觀察。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 中國國家主席習近平於 9 月 23 日至 25 日訪美並與川普舉行峰會 ， 雙方
同意延長貿易休兵至 2027 年 1 月 10 日，推動部分商品降稅、建立 AI
對話機制及持續磋商稀土供應議題，並延續高層溝通。台灣議題未列入
成果清單，關稅、科技競爭及供應鏈等核心分歧仍未化解，後續談判發
展仍待觀察。 
短評 
⚫ 美國 9 月 PMI 強勁擴張，顯示企業活動與就業動能仍具韌性，但投入
成本與售價上升也反映通膨壓力升溫。相較之下，房市需求尚未全面恢
復，消費信心則明顯轉弱，顯示內需前景仍有隱憂；美中延長貿易休兵
雖有助穩定市場，但核心分歧未解，後續不確定性仍高。

元 
區 
9 月景氣增強，進一步推高升息預期 
經濟 ⚫ 歐元區 9 月綜合採購經理人指數(PMI)增 1.1 點至 53.1，服務業 PMI 增 1.4 點至 53，製造業 PMI 則持平於 52.7。

⚫ 儘管 9 月經濟表現優於預期，但通膨壓力也同步升溫，再加上先前歐洲
央行的鷹派態度，使得 10 月升息一碼的可能性些微增加，亦強化未來
升息預期。

予中國稀土供應履約的壓力。 
⚫ 美中每年貿易總額逾 4,000 億美元，且降稅產品並非半導體、先進製程
設備、電動車、軍民兩用技術等關鍵領域，本次降稅實質效益有限。

⚫ 亞銀預估 2026 年東協國家 GDP 為 4.7%，其中越南 GDP 達 7.8%表現
最佳。 
重要 
事件 ⚫ 印尼央行 9 月會議第三度維持基準利率於 5.75%不變。 
短評 ⚫ AI 驅動的科技投資仍是亞洲經濟體最重要的成長動能來源，而能源、 糧食及氣候風險將成為影響各國通膨與信用風險的重要變數。

一、 美國—PMI 大幅走升、新屋銷售回穩，消費信心薄弱，美中關係暫現緩和
訊號 
標普全球 (S&P Global) 公布， 2026 年 9 月美國採購經理人綜合指數 (PMI 
Composite Output Index)初值由上月的 56.0 升至 58.4， 連續第四個月攀升 ， 創2021
年 7 月以來新高；其中服務業採購經理人指數(Services PMI)由 56.5 升至 58.7，創
59 個月高點，製造業採購經理人指數(Manufacturing PMI) 由 53.9 升至 57.0，創
2022 年 5 月以來最高水準，顯示美國企業活動持續強勁擴張。受國內市場需求穩
健帶動，製造業與服務業新訂單同步加速成長，推升整體產出增速升至逾 5 年來
最快水準，企業亦積極擴增人力，就業成長速度創 2022 年 6 月以來新高。另一
方面，積壓訂單增加及供應鏈延遲加劇，反映產能利用率持續攀升；加以近期油
價上漲推升燃料及運輸成本，使企業投入成本增幅升至 2022 年 10 月以來最高，
帶動售價漲幅回升，顯示通膨壓力有所升溫。儘管如此，企業對未來一年營運前
景仍維持正向看法，反映市場對美國經濟韌性及需求持續擴張仍具信心。 
2026 年 8 月美國新屋銷售經季節性調整年化率為 68.4 萬戶 ， 較上月增加6.4%，
但較去年同期下降 2.0%，顯示新屋銷售較前月回升，惟仍低於去年同期水準。地
區別表現分歧，其中中西部及南部分別較上月增加 84.9%及 6.9%，東北部及西部
則分別減少 36.1%及 15.2%。價格方面，8 月新屋銷售中位價為 39.37 萬美元，較
上月小升 0.4%，但較去年同期下降 5.8%。供給方面，8 月底經季節性調整後待售
新屋數量為 48.3 萬戶，與上月持平，較去年同期減少 2.0%；按目前銷售速度估
算，供給月數為 8.5 個月，低於上月的 9.0 個月，與去年同期大致持平。整體而
言，8 月美國新屋銷售較前月回升，主要受中西部與南部銷售增加帶動，惟仍低
於去年同期，顯示需求動能尚未完全恢復。價格方面，中位價較去年同期下滑，
反映市場價格仍有調整壓力；同時待售新屋維持在 48 萬戶以上，供給月數仍達
8.5 個月，顯示整體供給相對充裕，後續仍須觀察銷售回升是否具持續性。 
密西根大學公布，2026 年 9 月美國消費者信心指數終值降至 48.1，低於上月
的 51.7， 月減7.0%、 年減12.7%， 為近四個月低點 ， 並較2026 年 1 月下降約 15%，
顯示消費者信心持續轉弱。分項來看，當前經濟狀況指數由51.9 降至 50.9，月減

1.9%、 年減15.7%； 消費者預期指數則由51.5 降至 46.3， 月減10.1%、 年減10.4%，
反映民眾對未來景氣與個人財務前景的看法明顯惡化。調查指出，消費者對高物
價的擔憂持續升高，當前與未來一年個人財務看法均較上月轉弱約一成；短期企
業展望也因燃料價格偏高與貿易爭端再度升溫而明顯下滑。政治立場方面，各陣
營受訪者普遍認為年初以來經濟前景轉弱，其中共和黨人信心較 1 月下降 20%，
民主黨人下降 13%。 通膨預期方面 ， 未來一年通膨預期由8 月的 4.0%升至 4.6%，
為今年 6 月以來最高，且高於今年 2 月的 3.4%(美伊爆發衝突之前)及 2024 年各
月水準；長期通膨預期亦由 3.3%升至 3.4%，結束連續三個月持平，並高於 2024
年 2.8%至 3.2%的區間。整體而言，9 月美國消費信心再度降溫，顯示高物價、燃
料價格與貿易不確定性持續壓抑民眾信心，後續仍須觀察通膨預期升溫是否進一
步影響消費動能。 
中國國家主席習近平於 2026 年 9 月 23 日至 25 日訪問美國，並於 9 月 24 日
在華府白宮與美國總統川普舉行峰會。會後，美中雙方未發布聯合聲明，而是分
別公布成果清單，顯示雙方在穩定雙邊關係及維持高層溝通方面取得部分共識，
包括同意建構「基於尊重、公平、對等的建設性戰略穩定關係」、推動 300 億美
元非敏感商品對等降稅安排、建立人工智慧 (AI)或「超級智慧」對話與風險溝通
機制，以及持續就稀土與關鍵礦產供應鏈議題進行磋商。此外，美方並表示中國
將於 2027 年及 2028 年分別進口至少 1,000 萬公噸美國煤炭。值得注意的是，台
灣議題並未出現在雙方公布的成果文件中；另一方面，中方則額外強調兩軍危機
溝通機制及合作搜尋失蹤美軍遺骸等事項。整體而言，本次川習會有助延續美中
對話與降低短期緊張情勢，惟雙方在關稅、科技競爭、供應鏈安全及其他戰略議
題上的結構性分歧仍未完全化解，後續談判進展及雙邊互動方向仍有待持續觀察。 
二、歐元區—9 月景氣增強，進一步推高升息預期 
 歐元區 9 月綜合採購經理人指數(PMI)增 1.1 點至 53.1，且為連續四個月上
升。其中服務業PMI 增 1.4 點至 53，創近 10 個月高點，主要反映 AI 與國防投資
活動增加，帶動商業服務需求，抵銷能源價格上漲對消費購買力的負面影響。製
造業 PMI 則持平於 52.7，不過，德國萊茵河水位下降，沿岸較為發達的化工、製
藥業物流成本增加，因而暫時壓抑製造業上行空間。值得注意的是，由於國際原
油價格回升，綜合投入、產出物價指數皆上升至近 4 個月最高。故儘管 9 月經濟
表現優於預期，但通膨壓力也同步升溫，再加上先前歐洲央行的鷹派態度，使得

三、日本—9 月綜合 PMI 維持擴張，製造業表現仍具韌性 
 日本 9 月綜合 PMI 由 53.5 降至 52.5，製造業與服務業同步回落，經濟成長動
能較前兩個月的高檔有所降溫。製造業 PMI 下跌 0.8 點至 54.1，雖然產出、新訂
單及出口訂單增速放緩，但外部需求仍相當強勁，尤其 AI 相關需求可能持續帶
動出口表現，加上企業開始重建庫存、供應鏈瓶頸尚未完全解除，預料補庫存需
求將在未來數月繼續支撐生產成長。服務業 PMI 下降 0.9 點至 51.6，受到工資、
運輸及能源成本上升影響 ， 需求動能略有降溫 ， 但積壓訂單與出口業務有所改善 。
另，投入成本與售價漲幅均較前期放緩，顯示通膨壓力正逐步緩解，但整體物價
水準仍維持高檔。此外，企業對未來景氣的信心持續改善，未來活動指數升至七
個月高點，反映企業普遍預期經濟擴張趨勢仍可望延續。 
四、中國—美中貿易延長休戰，惟後續協商成效仍待觀察 
1-8 月工業部門營業收入、利潤年增率分別為 6.6%、17.6%，其中營業收入年
增率較上期(1-7 月)略增 0.1 個百分點，但利潤年增率下降 1.9 個百分點。受惠於
金屬、塑化原物料上漲，加上 AI 需求擴張，以及政府推動高科技產業發展等因
素，營收成長貢獻度較高者包含有色金屬加工(營收、利潤年增 31.1%、71.8%)、
計算機、通信和其他電子設備製造業(營收、利潤年增20.1%、109.9%)、化學原料
及製品(營收、利潤年增 9.1%、51%)。此外，電氣機械及器材業、汽車業營收年
增率雖分別為 11.5%、2.9%，但獲利分別年減 5.2%、16%。非金屬礦物製品業營
收、獲利下滑 9.5、46.7%，黑色金屬加工業營收、獲利分別年減 1.1%、62.4%，
反映產業持續進行減產所致。整體而言，部分產業受惠於短期原物料價格波動，
以及受到政策推動的高科技產業表現較佳，惟部分產業因陷入產能調整週期，或
政府減少支持等因素而表現較弱，產業分化明顯。 
展望未來，儘管美中雙方互相降低等值 300 億美元產品關稅，惟考量雙方每
年貿易總額逾 4,000 億美元，且降稅產品並非半導體、先進製程設備、電動車、
軍民兩用技術等關鍵領域，因此實質效益有限。另外，雖然美中雙方同意延長貿
易休戰，但休戰期只延長兩個月，反映美國仍保留談判槓桿，持續給予中國稀土
供應履約的壓力。後續將關注美中能否在休戰期間就開放市場、科技競爭、供應

鏈安全等議題取得進一步共識，若談判不如預期，美國仍可能重新祭出新的反制
措施 ， 而中國亦可能運用稀土作為談判籌碼 ， 經貿關係不確定性仍是較高 。 此外 ，
預計 10 月 8 日舉行歐中貿易會談，若談判未能取得突破，歐盟對中國的貿易限
制恐進一步升級，對中國出口形成額外壓力。 
五、印度及東南亞—亞銀上修亞洲及太平洋經濟體 2026 年 GDP 預估至 4.7% 
亞 洲 開 發 銀 行 (ADB) 最 新 經 濟 展 望 (Asian Development Outlook, ADO 
September 2026 )上修 2026 年亞洲及太平洋地區經濟體 2026 年經濟成長率(GDP)
至 4.7%(7 月預估 4.5%)、2027 年為 4.5%。ADB 認為，亞洲開發中經濟體雖面臨
中東衝突延長、俄烏戰爭再升溫、能源價格高檔震盪及強烈聖嬰現象等逆風，但
在政府刺激政策、民間投資及 AI 帶動科技出口支撐下，區域經濟仍展現韌性。
通膨方面，2026 年通膨率下修至 3.9%，惟能源與糧食價格壓力仍高，2027 年通
膨預測上修至 3.3%。 
區域經濟表現方面 ， 南亞成長最強勁 ，2026 年 GDP 成長率預估上修至 6.4%，
主要受印度內需與投資帶動；東亞則受中國內需疲弱影響，成長維持平穩；東協
國家(ASEAN)上調至 4.7%， 其中越南 、 馬來西亞及泰國受惠於科技與製造業投資
而獲上修。中亞及西亞、太平洋地區則面臨能源及食品價格上漲壓力，成長預測
略為下修至 3.7%、3.0%。(詳下表) 
ADB 認為未來最大風險包括地緣政治衝突升級、聖嬰 現象造成糧食供應衝
擊，以及 AI 相關資產可能出現價格重估。整體而言，AI 驅動的科技投資仍是亞
洲經濟體最重要的成長動能來源，而能源、糧食及氣候風險將成為影響各國通膨
與信用風險的重要變數。

五、台灣—AI 浪潮推升接單動能，外銷訂單再創新高 
8 月外銷訂單達 1,029.6 億美元，首度突破千億美元大關，其中受惠於 ASIC
晶片需求帶動，資通產品接單翻倍、電子產品接單亦大增 83.9%，惟光學器材因
面板需求維持低檔，年減 5%。非科技產品方面，基本金屬年增 26.8%，係因銅箔
及銅箔基板受惠 AI 相關供應鏈需求及銅價續漲，加以鍍鋅鋼需求回溫；機械年
增 28.3%，係因半導體業者增加資本支出，帶動半導體設備、自動化設備需求延
續；化學品年增 15.4%，主因藥品接單成長，加上產業用化學品需求持續增加；
塑橡膠製品年增 6.7%， 主係塑膠原料受原料上漲而推升產品售價 ， 加上部分塑膠
製品訂單回溫。展望未來，經濟部統計處預估 2026 年外銷訂單有望突破 1 兆美
元，在 AI 相關需求持續擴張帶動下，科技產品表現可望維持強勁，而傳統產業
中下游產品亦可望受惠於相關供應鏈需求，惟上游原物料價格競爭激烈，後續仍
須關注第四季消費旺季是否有相關備貨需求。

2026 年・冬のボーナス予測 
～前年比＋3.0％と６年連続の増加を予想～ 
 経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト  新家 義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

○ 民間企業の2026 年冬のボーナス一人当たり支給額を前年比＋3.0％（昨冬同＋2.8％）と予想する
（厚生労働省「毎月勤労統計」ベース）。冬のボーナスとしては６年連続の増加となるだろう。中
東情勢の悪化に伴う資源価格上昇が企業活動の重荷となるなかでも企業収益は高水準を維持してい
る。人手不足を背景とした処遇改善の動きも根強く、今冬もボーナスの増加が続くと予想する。

○ 11 月６日に厚生労働省から公表された26 年夏のボーナス（賞与支給事業所における労働者一人平
均賞与額）は、前年比＋5.3％と５年連続で増加し、昨夏の同＋2.9％から伸びも拡大した。また、
賞与を支給していない事業所も含む「全事業所における労働者一人平均賞与額」で見ても同＋
5.9％と増加している。賞与を支給する事業所の割合も前年の73.4％から74.0％へ上昇しており、
企業の従業員への還元姿勢が強いことがうかがえる。

○ ボーナスの交渉では、春闘時にその年の年間賞与を決定する夏冬型が採用されていることが多い。
2026 年春闘では、月例給与だけでなく一時金でも増額回答が目立った。企業収益が高水準にある
ことに加え、物価高への配慮や人材確保の観点から、賞与を含めた処遇改善を進める企業が多かっ
たとみられる。また、月例給与の引き上げが賞与の算定基礎額を押し上げ、支給月数の増加と相ま
ってボーナス増につながる面もある。夏のボーナス増に続き、冬についてもこうした交渉結果が反
映される形で増加が予想される。

○ 毎期型の企業、あるいは労働組合が存在しない中小・零細企業においては、より直近の収益状況・
業況がボーナスに反映されやすい。もっとも、イラン情勢悪化によるコスト上昇が企業収益の下押
し要因となるなかでも、価格転嫁の進展などを背景として企業収益は総じて底堅く推移している。
利益の水準も高く、従業員への還元余力はなお保たれているとみてよいだろう。

○ 人手不足感が強いことも、ボーナス増加を後押しする。企業にとって人材の確保・定着は引き続き
重要な経営課題となっており、月例給与だけでなく賞与を含めた処遇改善の必要性は高い。物価高
によって家計負担が増加するなか、従業員への還元を求める声も強い。こうした点も今冬のボーナ
ス増加を支える要因となる。

○ 労務行政研究所が９月30 日に公表した調査によれば、東証プライム上場企業175 社の2026 年年末

2/2 
一時金妥結額は、前年比＋4.2％と５年連続で増加した。製造業が同＋4.3％、非製造業が同＋
3.5％と、いずれもプラスとなっている。大企業中心の調査であり、中小企業やパートタイム労働
者も含む毎月勤労統計とは対象が異なるものの、冬のボーナスも堅調に増加するという見方を裏付
ける結果といえる。

○ 冬のボーナス増加は家計の所得環境にとって好材料であり、個人消費を下支えすることが期待され
る。一方で懸念されるのが物価上昇の影響である。中東情勢悪化に伴うコスト上昇の価格転嫁など
を背景として、今後は物価上昇率が高まる可能性が高く、政府による電気・ガス代支援の効果を織
り込んでも、2027 年初にはＣＰＩコアの前年比上昇率が＋３％台に達することが予想される。物
価上昇による家計の購買力の圧迫が続くなか、ボーナス増加による個人消費の押し上げ効果につい
ては過度な期待は禁物だろう。

2308 台達電(Delta)

報告日期： 2026/11/02 研究員：曾智楷 (Kevin Tseng)  
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 3Q26 營收 1970.6 億 ， 毛利率34.1%， 營利率16.6%，EPS 9.04
元。受到供應鏈缺料以及拉貨遞延影響， 3Q26 毛利率不如預
期。  
 預估 4Q26 營收將維持成長趨勢，毛利率部分，隨著隨著庫存
預計開始去化，以及部分產品漲價部分產品為反映原物料成本
而調漲價格，毛利率將有望回升。  
 HVDC 部分， ±400V 已在下半年開始出貨， 800V 系統亦持續
發展推進，同時液冷、電源機櫃、 DC-DC 模組等新產品線持續
放量；公司亦持續推進固態氧化物燃料電池 (SOFC) ，目前已有
海外大型客戶接觸送樣，初期產能逾 100MW ，預計 2027 年正
式量產。

一、受缺料影響， 3Q26 毛利率 34.1% ，EPS 9.04 元 
台達電 3Q26 營收 1970.6 億， QoQ +7.5% ，YoY +31.1% ，略低
於公司預期，主要由於供應鏈缺料以及資料中心建置延遲，導致
部分貨品出貨遞延 。 就產品組合來看 ，3Q26 電源及零部件 (Power 
Electronics) 佔整體營收的 54% ，交通 (Mobility) 佔 5% ，自動化
(Automation) 佔 8% 基礎建設 (Infrastructure) 佔 33% 。而獲利方
面， 3Q26 營業毛利 672 億， QoQ +2.8% ，YoY +28.1% ，毛利率
34.1% ，QoQ -1.6ppts ，YoY -0.8ppts ，公司表示，第三季存貨增
加，認列約 39 億元的備抵存貨跌價損失。主要由於現有產品缺
料，以及新產品客戶新平台量產較慢、拉貨延後所致，預計第四
季至明年第一季陸續消化並回沖。 3Q26 營業淨利 326.2 億，營利
率 16.6% ，業外淨利 10.46 億，稅後淨利 234.95 億，單季稅後
EPS 9.04 元。

二、庫存去化、部分產品漲價， 4Q26 營運有望回升  
展望 4Q26 ，公司表示，第四季營收確定將維持成長趨勢，而毛利
率部分，公司亦預估，第四季毛利率將優於第三季，主要由於隨
著庫存預計從第四季開始去化，並在明年第一季陸續消化完畢，
而存貨順利出貨後即可回沖。另外，第四季部分產品為反映原物
料成本而調漲價格 。 預估4Q26 營收 2181 億，QoQ +10.7% ，YoY 
+35.0% ，毛利率 35.5% 。營利率 18.2% ，稅後淨利 283 億，單季
稅後 EPS 10.89 元。

三. 預估 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 37.53/62.49 元 
展望 2026 年，公司表示， 2026 年全年整體資料中心相關營收佔
比將接近 5 成，其中， AI 電源解決方案營收佔比有望達 25% ，液
冷散熱則大約佔 13% 。預估 2026 全年營收 7574 億元， YoY 
+36.5% ，毛利率 35.5% ，營利率 17.3% ，稅後淨利 974.8 億，全
年稅後 EPS 37.53 元。展望 2027 年，受惠於 CSP 持續擴大資本
支出， AI 資料中心建置需求延續，公司預期， AI 相關營收與液冷
系統產品 2027 年的營收佔比將進一步攀升。 HVDC 部分， ±400V
已在下半年開始出貨 ， 由NVIDIA 推動的 800V 系統亦持續發展推
進，同時液冷、電源機櫃、 DC-DC 模組等新產品線持續放量，此
外，公司亦持續推進固態變壓器 (SST) 與固態氧化物燃料電池
(SOFC) ，初期產能逾 100MW ，預計 2027 年正式量產，目前已有
海外大型客戶接觸送樣。而產能部分，公司亦宣布，將於馬來西
亞檳城設立大型生產據點，預計五年內產能追上泰國廠區。另外，
針對 Vicor 提出侵權訴訟以及美國 ITC 啟動調查，公司則表示，
公司自身擁有多項 IP，且 ITC 程序冗長，短期內對公司出貨與客
戶不會造成影響。預估 2027 全年營收 1 兆 695 億元， YoY 
+41.2% ，毛利率 36.5% ，營利率 20.0% ，稅後淨利 1623 億，全
年稅後 EPS 62.49 元。

四. 投資建議為「買進」，目標價 1680 元 
考量到 CSP 持續擴大資本支出， AI 資料中心建置需求延續，公司
具備關鍵產業地位，且漲價效益及遞延訂單後續將陸續反映，將
有望持續挹注公司營收及獲利成長動能。以公司 2027 年 EPS 來
看，目前股價交易於 2027 年 EPS 約 21~22 倍本益比，評價仍具
上修空間，給於買進投資建議，目標價 1680 元(27x 2027 EPS) 。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介  
台達電 (Delta) 成立於 1971 年，以製造繞線式磁性元件起家，早期
專注於筆電 (NB) 、桌機 (DT) 、螢幕 (Monitor) 、伺服器 (Server) 、印
表機 (Printer) 及網通等 IT 相關產品之電源供應器與散熱風扇，憑
藉深厚的核心技術延伸發展，穩居全球磁性與散熱元件重要供應
商及全球最大交換式電源供應器龍頭。因應全球電子產業轉變與
環保節能意識抬頭，台達電近年積極跨足工業自動化、智慧安防
及綠能領域，逐步從關鍵零組件製造商轉型為「整體節能解決方
案提供者」，成為全球電源管理與散熱解決方案的專業領導廠商。
目前集團深耕電源及零組件、自動化與基礎設施等核心業務，並
進一步拓展至交通領域，依 2025 年產品結構比重來看，分別為電

二.產業趨勢概況  
1. 2026 年全球 AI 伺服器出貨量預計年增 31% 
根據 TrendForce 最新 AI server 產業研究，近期超大型 CSP 、
Tier-2 資料中心業者對 NVIDIA GB/VR 等整櫃式 AI server 方案的
採購意願明顯提高，且 Google 、AWS 新一代 ASIC 平台於 2026
下半年陸續放量，加上中系業者擴大採用本地 AI 方案以滿足雲端
大型語言模型 (LLM) AI 需求，促使 TrendForce 上調 2026 年 AI 
server 出貨年成長幅度，從原先的 28% 更新為近 31%。北美五大
業者 2026 年的資本支出多翻倍提升，反映出大型 CSP 廠持續擴
建 AI 資料中心、 GPU 叢集、液冷散熱等新一代基礎設施，以應對
生成式 AI 及大型模型快速成長帶來的算力需求。其中， Google 
2026 至 2027 年都將側重自家 TPU 晶片發展， 2027 年出貨量有
望繼續高速成長。 AWS 2026 年以 NVIDIA GB300 作為 GPU AI 
server 主力，自研 ASIC 也穩定出貨，預期至 2027 年量能將再擴
大。 Meta 2026 年主要採用 NVIDIA GB/VR 、AMD Helios 整櫃式
方案，自研 ASIC 布局預計至 2027 年將明顯加速，為該公司長期
降低 AI 基礎設施成本、提升推論效率的重要策略。

根據 TrendForce 最新研究顯示，隨著 AI 基礎設施建置浪潮及高
階晶片運算功率不斷攀升，單晶片熱設計功耗已超越 1kW ，使液

3. 預估 2026 年全球資料中心用電需求年增 31% 
根據  TrendForce 產業研究顯示 ， 受惠於AI 應用需求激增與雲端
服務供應商 (CSP) 加速擴建基礎設施 ， 全球資料中心電力需求容量
預計將由 2025 年的 122.9GW 攀升至 2026 年的 161GW ，YoY 
+31% ，並於 2027 年維持超過 30% 的高成長率；其中， AI 伺服器
為核心增長動能，占整體電力需求比重由 2025 年的 25% 提升至
2026 年的 33.4% ，預計 2027 年更將突破 40% ，同步帶動散熱與
供電等非 IT 設備需求，促使資料中心規劃導入高壓直流供電
(HVDC) 以優化供配電效率。然而，電網供電增速卻未能跟上需求
擴張 ， 全球資料中心電力供需自2026 年起開始背離 ， 並將於2028
年後因電網併網與輸配電建設交付遞延而顯著擴大；預估至 2030
年，全球資料中心電力需求將達 490.7GW ，但電網可供容量僅
222.6GW ，供需缺口高達 268GW( 其中資料中心部署量最大的美
國自 2028 年起遞延加劇，至 2030 年缺口預計超過 170GW) ，未
來此一實質短缺與帳面落差能否透過業者自建發電及電網改善來
填補，仍有待觀察。

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估

一. 庫存去化、部分產品漲價， 4Q26 營運有望回升  
展望 4Q26 ，公司表示，第四季營收確定將維持成長趨勢，而毛利
率部分，公司亦預估，第四季毛利率將優於第三季，主要由於隨
著庫存預計從第四季開始去化，並在明年第一季陸續消化完畢，
而存貨順利出貨後即可回沖。另外，第四季部分產品為反映原物
料成本而調漲價格 。 預估4Q26 營收 2181 億，QoQ +10.7% ，YoY 
+35.0% ，毛利率 35.5% 。營利率 18.2% ，稅後淨利 283 億，單季
稅後 EPS 10.89 元。

二. 預估 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 37.53/62.49 元 
展望 2026 年，公司表示， 2026 年全年整體資料中心相關營收佔
比將接近 5 成，其中， AI 電源解決方案營收佔比有望達 25% ，液
冷散熱則大約佔 13% 。預估 2026 全年營收 7574 億元， YoY 
+36.5% ，毛利率 35.5% ，營利率 17.3% ，稅後淨利 974.8 億，全
年稅後 EPS 37.53 元。展望 2027 年，受惠於 CSP 持續擴大資本
支出， AI 資料中心建置需求延續，公司預期， AI 相關營收與液冷
系統產品 2027 年的營收佔比將進一步攀升。 HVDC 部分， ±400V
已在下半年開始出貨 ， 由NVIDIA 推動的 800V 系統亦持續發展推
進，同時液冷、電源機櫃、 DC-DC 模組等新產品線持續放量，此
外，公司亦持續推進固態變壓器 (SST) 與固態氧化物燃料電池
(SOFC) ，初期產能逾 100MW ，預計 2027 年正式量產，目前已有
海外大型客戶接觸送樣。而產能部分，公司亦宣布，將於馬來西
亞檳城設立大型生產據點，預計五年內產能追上泰國廠區。另外，
針對 Vicor 提出侵權訴訟以及美國 ITC 啟動調查，公司則表示，
公司自身擁有多項 IP，且 ITC 程序冗長，短期內對公司出貨與客

### Page 1

MediaTek

TPU wins on track for steep ramp through 
’28 joined by further CSP/AI lab potential

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 6,200 TWD | Price: 3,915 TWD

3Q26 beat on consumer builds; 4Q26 lifted by TPU ramp 
3Q26 sales were +10% QoQ on its flagship mobile SoC launch and customers’ pre-builds 
ahead of its chipset price hike on rising manufacturing cost. On higher sales and better 
mix, GM/OpM edged up to 46%/16%, taking 3Q26 EPS to NT$17.5 above BofAe/street. 
4Q26 sales was guided +25-32% QoQ, in line with BofA preview, as steep TPUv8t ramp 
now tracking to US$2.5bn (vs. US$2bn guide before) partially balanced by soft demand 
elsewhere (-15-22% QoQ vs. 5-year seasonal +1%) following the early pull-in. Despite 
TPUv8t growing to ~35% of its sales at lower margins, GMs is guided resiliently at 45% 
in 4Q26, implying better margins for its merchant business at 47%. Fast sales scale-up 
and stable GMs should lift operating leverage with OpMs guided for 19% at midpoint. 
ASIC pipeline spanning across CSPs and frontier AI labs 
Mediatek sees TPUv8t ramping to US$16bn+ revenue in ’27, lifting OpM to 20%+, with 
upside if it could secure more capacity. The follow on TPUv9 is tracking for a meaningful 
production potential in ’28. Importantly, it confirmed a 2nd ASIC customer win in design 
now and production contribution set to kick off in ’28. We believe this new project would 
require Mediatek’s component sourcing and supply chain management at a much higher 
dollar content. This along with potential Bytedance win could add upside to our base 
case for its data center revenue of US$19bn/US$53bn in '27/'28 with xAI ASIC revenue 
joined from 2H28 to supplement Google TPU upgrades.  
AI project diversification supports earnings and valuation 
As ASIC business inflects, we lift our 2026 EPS to NT$74.13 and keep ‘27/28 EPS at 
NT$168.93/404.32. Keep our Buy rating and NT$6,200 PO on 24x 2H27-1H28E P/E 
(implied 30x ASIC EPS + 10x merchant EPS), backed by sales/EPS 90%/134% CAGR. 
Growing AI project wins should alleviate the concerns on customer concentration and 
TPU competition debate with share downside protected by 23x/10x ‘27/28 straight P/E.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
MediaTek is a fabless IC design company supplying to 
wireless communications and digital consumer markets. 
Before taking leadership in mobile SoC area, it had built 
solid ground in the TV SoC and optical disk drive markets. 
The firm invests heavily in R&D to seek better positioning 
in the 5G cycle, and has a complete offering including ASIC, 
Wi-Fi, PMIC etc. to address applications beyond 
smartphone, such as IoT and auto.

Investment Rationale 
We have Buy rating on MediaTek as we think MediaTek is 
ready and has the strength to brace for the upcoming 
proliferation of IoT devices and opportunities in growing 
markets like ASIC, ARM-based PC, auto, and etc., and we 
believe its solid balance sheet and cashflow could provide 
downside support to the share price. Meanwhile, we expect 
5G smartphone penetration to be a multi-year theme for 
the company, with its better competitive landscape in 
duopoly 5G era and an improved margin profile.

### Page 4

Price objective basis & risk 
MediaTek (MDTKF) 
Our PO of NT$6,200 is based on 24x 2H27-1H28 P/E. which is towards the higher end 
of its historical P/E range of 8-25x, as we believe the company's broadening ASIC 
pipeline could drive an earnings upgrade cycle.

Upside risks to our PO are: (1) better than expected smartphone demand, especially in 
China, (2) a better pricing in mobile communication segment thanks to alleviated 
competition and rising exposure to high-end/flagship market, and (3) an even more 
superior investment return on non-op side to uplift its net cash position. (4) Unexpected 
collaboration with global technology leaders leads to higher than expected 
margins/profits

Downside risks to our PO are: (1) weaker-than-expected end-market situation in 
consumer-related smartphone/TV space, (2) macro challenges including inflation/policy-
related pressures, (3) rising competition from existing players and/or newcomers in 
smartphone/TV SoC (system on chip) space, and (4) ASP/margin pressure caused by each 
or all of the above and/or rising foundry costs. (4) Bidding process for Meta Inference 
ASIC is slower than expected.

of potential price fluctuation, are: A - Low, B - Medium and C - High. INVESTMENT RATINGS reflect the analyst’s assessment of both a stock’s absolute total return 
potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment ratings are: 1 - Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment 
rating of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things, 
the 0-12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm’s guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a 
stock should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst’s view of the potential price 
appreciation (depreciation).

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
In this report, we focus on China's manufacturing PMI, which we
see as a potential catalyst for AirTAC's and Hiwin's share prices.
China's manufacturing PMI fell to 49.3 in October from 50.1 in September:

• This came in lower than both our estimate and consensus of 49.9.
• The production index decreased 1.9ppt while the new order index declined
1.1ppt MoM.
Our view:
• We believe the soft manufacturing PMI data could weigh on market
sentiment in the near term. Nonetheless, we prefer supply players with
company-specific drivers such as Airtac and Hiwin:
° Airtac (1590.TW, OW) should continue to benefit from its own share
gains and incremental contribution from linear guides. Valuation appears

undemanding at 16x 2027e P/E, vs. the 25x average since 2020 (see our
30 Oct 2026 report).
° Hiwin (2049.TW, OW) should show more margin expansion in coming
quarters, supported by higher utilization and price hikes. We see
potential share price upside at the current 28x 2027e P/E, vs. peak cycle
valuation of 35-40x.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of

Valuation Methodology and Risks
AirTAC International (1590.TW)
Base case, 29x 2027e P/E. We believe this methodology better captures earnings growth
momentum for industrial automation component plays (market sentiment moves along
with business cycles). As the industry enters an up-cycle, we think applying a near-peak cycle
valuation of 29x P/E is reasonable given the stronger business outlook.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger macroeconomic climate in China

n Stronger-than-expected demand from industrial automation
Risks to Downside
n Sharper economic downturn in China
n Miniature linear guideway business development taking more time
n Larger-than-expected investment in new products but weaker-than-expected demand
Hiwin Technologies Corp. (2049.TW)
Base case, P/E. We think this valuation methodology better captures Hiwin's earnings growth
momentum, as its valuation tends to move with the business cycle. We apply a target multiple

of 38x to our 2027 EPS estimate. We view this multiple as justified by the 47% operating profit
CAGR we project for 2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger industrial automation demand pickup.
n Market share gains.
n Earlier-than-expected revenue contribution from humanoids.
Risks to Downside
n Weaker industrial automation demand.
n More pricing pressure.
n Market share loss.
n More broad-based impact on the global economy from geopolitical risks.

ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Idea
2027 price hike should be case by case amid push back from some customers:
Management said 2027 pricing negotiations could be mixed across customers even
though capacity is nearly fully loaded. Some need more capacity in 4Q26 and are
willing to pay the new post-hike wafer price. However, the company still faces push
back from some customers, likely tied to their inventories. Our recent checks
suggest  Tier 1 foundry L TA wafer pricing into 2027 could be less than 10%, below

the buyside bogey of a 25-30% blended hike. However, for some tier 2 customers,
the price hike could indeed be around 25-30%. We believe the average like-to-like
L TA wafer price could only increase by  15-20% in 2027. Stay EW.
SOI demand exceeds expectation: The company said 12-inch SOI demand already
exceeds its current expansion plan. The priority is to debottleneck and ramp existing
Missouri capacity. More than half of the newly installed tools have qualified for

volume production, and RF SOI and silicon-photonics products have entered volume
production.
4Q26 could be relatively muted: We expect 4Q26 to be relatively muted since
wafer pricing remains tied to older agreements, existing capacity is fully loaded, and
new capacity depreciation would continue to weigh on margin.
With this report, Lucas Wang assumes coverage of GlobalWafers.

Still Better Supply/Demand Dynamics into 2027-28e
3Q26 earnings and 4Q26  outlook review: 3Q26 results missed both our and Visible
Alpha (VA) consensus estimates given the July fire at the Novara, Italy facility, which
disrupted 8-inch wafer production and related SOI output. We expect 4Q26 to be
relatively muted since wafer pricing remains tied to older agreements, existing capacity is
fully loaded and new capacity depreciation would continue to weigh on margin. We had

expected 4Q26 gross margin to be around 25-26%, but we have now reduced that to 22-
23%.

Micron/GWC deal marks a new milestone for GWC
Micron Technology and GlobalWafers announced a deeper strategic partnership on July 9,

2026, centered on a 10-year supply agreement and US$500mn of strategic financing from
Micron to support GlobalWafers' new 12-inch silicon wafer facility in Sherman, Texas. On
its latest earnings call, GWC board proposed executing that financing as up to US$500
million of 10-year privately placed convertible bonds, with Micron as the sole subscriber,
carrying a 0.5% coupon. The agreement secures significant raw silicon wafer capacity for
Micron's long-term manufacturing plans, with the financing linked to the buildout of

GlobalWafers America's (GWA) Sherman facility.
GlobalWafers Chairwoman Doris Hsu described the agreement as the largest and longest
contract in the company's history. The two companies will also collaborate on next-
generation wafer technologies and process innovation. The Sherman plant is notable as
the only US-based source of advanced 12-inch wafers.
The agreement forms part of Micron's broader US$3bn commitment to strengthen the US
semiconductor supply chain and accompanies its target of manufacturing 40% of its

DRAM output domestically by the mid-2030s. We believe GWC is well positioned to

### Page 4

M
Idea
4
support ongoing foundry and memory capacity expansion in the US, not only for Micron
but also for TSMC and Samsung, which are already GWC's two largest customers. We
currently assume Micron/GWC L TA pricing could be US$70-80 on average for the polished
wafer (10-20% above current L TA pricing after discounts), reflecting the higher US cost
base. This would be broadly in line with, or slightly above, L TAs signed in 2021-22.
Current inventory levels remain higher than during the 2021-22 upcycle

when existing L TAs were signed
Notably, customer inventories of 12-inch wafers remain well above levels seen during the
previous industry upcycle. According to SUMCO's inventory trend data (see Exhibit 3 ),
inventory volumes and turnover days were near the lowest levels of the 2014-26 period
during 2021-22, reflecting a lean and highly utilized supply chain. By contrast, the current
cycle presents the opposite picture: shipment volumes are approaching record highs, but

customer inventories are also near record levels, with inventory days and absolute
inventory exceeding those seen even at the peak of the 2021-22 upcycle.
This backdrop differs meaningfully from the 2021-22 environment and, in our view,
supports a more moderate and delayed pricing recovery in 2027 rather than a repeat of
the broad-based increases seen during the prior cycle. In 2021-22, wafer makers held
significant pricing leverage as customer inventories sat near cycle lows despite record

shipment volumes. Fabs consumed nearly all purchased wafers with minimal inventory
buffers, creating a tight supply environment that enabled wafer manufacturers to
implement meaningful price increases across 12-inch wafers.
While shipment volumes currently remain elevated, customers also hold substantial
inventory. This provides a buffer against demand growth and moderate supply disruptions,
reducing the urgency to secure incremental wafer supply. Historically, this dynamic

weakens suppliers' pricing power. Until inventories normalize meaningfully, wafer makers
are likely to have less pricing leverage than they did entering the 2021-22 upcycle. Our
view is that 2027 L TA negotiations will focus primarily on removing current discounts
rather than driving nominal price increases. We broadly expect 2027 industry L TA pricing
increases of 15-20% in average, with Tier 2 customers' wafer price to be raised 25-30% but
Tier 1 customers would only see 5-15%.

Estimate Revisions
We cut our EPS estimates by 14%, 15% and 14% for 2026-28, respectively. The
reductions in our  forecasts primarily reflect lower wafer price hike to Tier 1 customers and
the margin pressure from overseas capacity ramp. However, we also lower our tax rate
assumption from 25% to 20% to reflect the subsidy from the US government.

Valuation Methodology
We lower our price target from NT$900 to NT$770: The price target change mostly
reflects our earnings forecast change over  2026-28. All of of key assumptions in our DCF
model are unchanged, including  a cost of equity of 11% (beta of 1.5, risk premium of 6%
and risk-free rate of 2%), an intermediate growth rate of 13%  and a terminal growth rate
of 4%.
Our bull- and bear-case values are also lowered, from  NT$1,140.00 to NT$975.00 and NT

$450.00 to NT$385.00, respectively, implying P/Es of 32x and 13x 2027e.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Document 176: 261105_daiwa_vpec-UG

Source PDF: all report pdf/261105_daiwa_vpec-UG.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: Following VPEC’s robust 3Q26 earnings release on 30 
October and our subsequent market research, we raise our forecasts and 
12M TP, premised upon: 1) a higher top-line growth, thanks to its faster 
capacity conversion to meet strong demand for AI optical communication 
(OC) devices; and 2) a higher substrate supply consigned by customers, 
helping margin. Our thesis remains intact for VPEC to enjoy strong growth 
from its share gains in the AI OC market, with broadened customer base 
and applications. Given increased jitters on AI bubble and macro issues 
post the US mid-term elections, we upgrade our rating one notch to Buy (1) 
and suggest investors take advantage of market volatility to bargain-hunt.

What’s the impact: Forecast changes. We raise our 2026-27E EPS by 3-
4% to factor in VPEC’s robust 3Q26 results and higher revenue growth in 
4Q26, on our latest market research. This is premised upon: 1) faster-than-
expected capacity conversion from GaAs for mobile to InP for AI OC at its 
MOCVD reactors to mitigate supply constraints and meet more optical 
demand; and 2) increased InP substrate supply consigned by customers, 
helping to increase GM. As such, we expect VPEC’s earnings growth to 
remain strong through 2027, supporting its hefty valuation.

Speeding up capacity conversion to meet more demand. Given a 
limited floor space in its Fab 2, VPEC may accommodate 70+ MOCVD 
reactors in 2027 (currently: 65). Yet, given still-suboptimal utilisation in its 
RF business due to weak consumer demand for smartphones on cost 
inflation, VPEC is accelerating capacity conversion to accommodate strong 
AI OC demand, in order to narrow the supply/demand gap.

Increasing substrate consignment to benefit GM. Due to the InP 
substrate supply constraint and strong AI demand, customers with strong 
purchasing power are increasing substrate consignment to VPEC, adding 
to GM since the consigned materials are excluded from the GM calculation. 
We continue to expect the company to register AI-related revenue, 
including PD, LD and HAMR, with broadened customer base, of TWD1.2-
1.3bn this year, TWD3bn in 2027E and at least TWD4bn in 2028E, from 
TWD550m in 2025 (see our preview note, 8 September).

What we recommend: We raise our 12M TP for VPEC to TWD555 
(previous: TWD535), still based on our 4-quarter-forward PER of 58x. We 
upgrade our rating by one notch to Buy (1), with key downside risk of 
China’s export control inducing any unexpected supply chain hiccups.

How we differ: Our 2027-28E EPS are 14-36% below Bloomberg 
consensus, as we believe the street is too aggressive on VPEC’s AI ramp.

5 November 2026

Upgrading: value resurfaces after jitters rise

➢ Raising forecasts on both higher top-line and margin trajectories 
➢ Intact optical ramp in AI connectivity despite market volatility 
➢ Upgrading our rating to Buy (1) with a higher 12M TP of TWD555

Company profile 
Founded in 1996, Visual Photonics Epitaxy (VPEC) is a veteran epiwafer maker in Taiwan. The 
company started with growing epiwafers for LED applications and then transitioned to microwave 
communications. Strategically moving up the technology verticals, in recent years VPEC has tapped into 
the high-value-add applications of optical sensing & communications for both blended ASP and margin 
accretion.

Executive/board
quality 2
VPEC's board of directors (BoD) has 11 members, including 3 independent directors,
representing 27% of total directors in 2023, which is slightly higher than the regulatory
requirement but lower than at other semiconductor companies under our coverage. Its chairman
and general manager are not the same person and most of the directors are not executive
management. In addition to the annual internal performance evaluation of the BoD, VPEC also
requests an external evaluation of its BoD at least once every 3 years. We believe this helps
strengthen the functionalities of the BoD despite a relatively low percentage of independent
directors.
Capital management 1
In the past 10 years, the company’s payout ratio has been above 78%. In terms of the dollar
amount, its cash dividends were TWD2.3 per share in 2017-23. We view VPEC has well-
managed dividend policy and see its dividend payout ratio is appropriate.
Related party &

VPEC has set up a "Supplier Management Procedure (M0047)" and evaluates its suppliers in
terms of environmental management, compliance, and green products. The company audits its
suppliers to ensure a good performance in environmental sustainability, health & safety and
corporate social responsibility.

2 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
The U.S. Capex Boom No One is Talking About—And Its Implications
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
We have long been of the belief that the U.S. is on the cusp of an inbound FDI capex boom
that can result in numerous positive spillover effects for the U.S. economy. The latest

figures from the U.S. Department of Commerce bear this out.
Exhibit 1 highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be inside the U.S.,
with FDI totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year. That is a staggering
103% jump from a year ago and places FDI inflows on track for a record year.
The latest inbound capex already adds to the massive scale of foreign investment in the
U.S. To wit, the accumulated stock of FDI in the U.S. stands at nearly $6T, based on the

latest figures from the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA). Manufacturing accounts for
roughly 43% of the total, and, at last count, some 10,000 foreign affiliates were operating
in the U.S.
These firms are largely invisible to the average U.S. investor, but the activities of U.S.
affiliates of foreign multinationals are substantial and competitive- and growth-enhancing.
To this point and based on the latest data from the BEA, foreign affiliates in the U.S.

employed nearly 8.6 million U.S. workers in 2024. That represents 6.1% of total private
sector employment, with manufacturing and retail the two most prominent sectors
of employment. Roughly 24% of all manufacturing jobs in the U.S. are from foreign
subsidiaries. In terms of pay, U.S. workers at these firms make $89,000 annually, 7% higher
than the national average, according to the Global Business Alliance.
Foreign affiliates also contributed some $1.5T to U.S. GDP; comprised some 15.7% of total

U.S. private sector investment; accounted for 12.4% of total U.S. research & development
(R&D) expenditures; paid roughly 20% of all U.S. federal taxes; and made up 22.5% of
U.S. exports and 26.2% of U.S. imports. 1 These are the direct effects of U.S. subsidiaries
of foreign companies operating in the U.S. The indirect effects are even greater in terms
of more income, jobs, growth and tax revenue—all bolstering the long-term competitive
advantage of the U.S. In a nutshell, foreign firms operating in the U.S. are an important

ingredient of U.S. economic growth.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Companies that once served the U.S. market exclusively through exports
increasingly face higher costs and more uncertainties and hence want to be “inside.”
Two, with a $32T-plus economy with extraordinary consumer purchasing power, foreign

firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment than just trade (exports). There
is no more dynamic consumer in the world than the American shopper.
Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers, semiconductors, power
generation, etc., has created numerous investment opportunities for many foreign firms
that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging capex cycle.
Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly developed

technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.
As more U.S. affiliates of foreign multinationals set up shop in the U.S., think more job
creation across the country, more manufacturing output, more locally sourced purchases,
more U.S. exports, more spending on R&D, and more tax revenue for local communities.
And over the long run, think a more globally competitive U.S. economy.
1 Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign Multinational Enterprises, 2024” and “Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign

Multinational Enterprises, 2023”, BEA July 2026.
Investment Implications
The boom in foreign investment in
the U.S. should continue to support
employment, productivity, corporate
earnings, and broader U.S. economic
competitiveness, in our view. Hence
our U.S. bias.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
And speaking of global competitiveness, Exhibit 2A is very illustrative of the magnet pull of
the U.S. economy to attract FDI versus the rest of the world. Of new greenfield investment
this decade, note the overriding preference for the U.S., accounting for nearly 16% of total
global greenfield investment this decade. America’s share is larger than the next three
countries combined—the United Kingdom, India, and Germany. And note that America’s

share of new investment is more than 5X that of China’s. Most of this new investment
has gone into manufacturing sectors like electrical equipment, computers, appliances and
batteries and has originated from Asia and Europe.
The bulk of foreign investment comes from Europe, followed by Japan and Canada, but as
Exhibit 2B highlights, there is a new dynamic playing out when it comes to America’s ability
to attract capital. There is a new source in town: a handful of developing nations (namely

Taiwan, South Korea, Mexico and Brazil). Mexican manufacturers, Taiwan technology
leaders, South Korea chip leaders, Brazil industrial firms, India pharmaceutical firms—these
companies and more are realizing that in today’s trade environment, the best way to serve
the American market is not necessarily through exports but through investment—investing
locally. FDI flows from developing nations reached $63B in the 12 months through June,
more than double the level a year prior ($24.5B), according to the BEA. Fueled by insatiable

AI demand, flows from South Korea alone totaled $23.4B in the first half of 2026. The
upshot is that an increasingly important source of capital for the U.S. is coming from an
unlikely source: the developing nations.
More fire for the capex boom. The coming boom in FDI inflows is supportive of our thesis
that the U.S. stands on the cusp of a major industrial upgrade. We are in the early innings
of a structural—not cyclical—capex cycle that should lead to the rebuilding of domestic

supply chains, higher value-added manufacturing, and more AI-driven productivity growth.
Foreign firms, in our opinion, will be part of this process.
That is important to recognize because remember the following: when a foreign company
builds a factory or an R&D facility or expands its existing manufacturing capacity right
here in the U.S., good things happen. Demand for U.S. goods and services goes up (as does
demand for construction, materials, logistics and professional services). New jobs are created,

boosting incomes of U.S. workers, while supporting more local spending and consumption.
And because many foreign firms often bring advanced manufacturing technologies when
investing in the U.S., the end results include a boost in productivity and innovation in various
sectors of the economy.
All the above, of course, are the secret ingredients to stronger U.S. economic growth, rising
corporate earnings and higher equity prices. With a little help from our friends, America’s

industrial renewal is gaining strength and momentum.

MARKET VIEW
Europe’s Electoral Cycle and Market Risks
Ehiwario Efeyini, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
As midterm elections approach for the U.S. next month, voters across Europe will also
enter their own election season starting this November and continuing throughout
2027. Over the next year, 52% of the total EU population, representing 46% of EU GDP
across eight countries, will go to the polls, including voters in three of its four largest

economies (Exhibit 3). Having announced snap general elections earlier this month,
Spain will vote for members of its lower and upper house on November 29. A series of
parliamentary elections are then due for 2027 in Estonia (by March 7), Finland (on April
18), Greece (by July 25), Slovakia (by September 28), Poland (by November 11) and Italy
(by December 22). And perhaps most consequentially France will stage Presidential
elections on April 18, with a potential second round of voting set for May 2.

For the EU as a whole, the significance of these coming elections will be the potential
for a shift toward more populist, domestically oriented policy priorities from leading
opposition candidates (particularly in France, Spain and Poland) which could obstruct the
process of closer EU integration. In the nearer term, adoption of the new seven-year
(2028-2034) EU budget due by the end of 2027 could be impeded. And over the longer
term, renewed efforts toward an EU-wide capital market union to deepen and attract

more inward investment into European markets could also be further delayed.
At the country level, investors have seen bond yields rise globally in 2026 on the back of
higher inflation and concerns over fiscal sustainability. The debt question has arguably
been most pressing in parts of Europe, especially given the likelihood that upcoming
elections make for domestic increases in government largesse. And of the eight
countries going to the polls over the next year, France remains the biggest source of

concern. Interest rate spreads on French 10-year sovereign bonds have overtaken those
in both Greece and Italy this year (Exhibit 4), driven by investor concerns over public
debt. The 2027 government budget unveiled earlier this month projected a deficit of
5.0% of GDP (well in excess of the 3% specified in the EU Stability and Growth Pact),
but remained insufficiently expansive to nullify the risk that the current government
could be unseated later this year if it is forced through the fragmented parliament. This

comes on top of an existing debt load of close to 120% of GDP and a halving of official
2026 growth forecasts from 1.0% at the start of the year to 0.5% in September. In
short, all four variables in France’s debt sustainability equation currently stand as major
headwinds, and the two leading candidates in the 2027 election have shown limited
appetite for fiscal consolidation over the coming years.

We remain cautious on European
markets overall, despite seeing
pockets of strength in selected
areas such as defense, data center
infrastructure and renewable energy.
On top of growing uncertainty
from the upcoming electoral cycle

and interest rate outlook, rising
competition and slowing demand
from China continue to challenge
key segments of the Consumer
Discretionary sector. And limited
direct AI concentration also leaves
Europe under-exposed to the key
growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and
emerging Asia.

Intervention from the ECB under its 2022 bond purchase program is also unlikely at this

stage given that the spread widening in France has not appeared either disorderly or
unwarranted. Meanwhile, conditions could potentially be aggravated by expected ECB rate
hikes in response to still above-target inflation across the eurozone. Indeed recent moves
to tighten monetary policy from a number of major central banks have been read most
hawkishly in Europe. Most of the increase in European bond yields has come from higher
inflation breakevens as opposed to real yields in the U.S. and Japan. And despite some

paring back of tightening expectations in the wake of recent bond volatility, the market is
still pricing three more policy rate increases over the next 12 months.
Euro depreciation and an emerging shift toward more protectionist trade policy with China
could further complicate the ECB policy outlook. China posted a record $35B trade surplus,
according to Eurostat, with the EU in September (alongside its $938B total surplus for the
first three quarters of 2026), and the two markets have agreed to reduce the EU bilateral

deficit by capping imports of hybrid cars for four years. Hybrids account for less than 2%
of total EU imports from China, but this could potentially mark the start of a broader shift
toward more trade protection in Europe with its largest foreign supplier which risks adding
further upward pressure to cost-push inflation. And though the leading candidates in the
French election may favor resuming oil and gas imports from Russia, Europe also remains

among the most at risk from the global energy shock. Indeed some 56% of the region’s
crude oil consumption is in the form of diesel product according to the International Energy
Agency, more than twice the U.S. share.
The 2- to 10-year eurozone yield curve has already flattened significantly since the start
of the year. And at just over 30 basis points as of mid-October, it remains flatter than
its equivalents in the U.S., UK and Japan. Outside Energy and Technology, Financials have

been among the strongest-performing sectors in Europe this year. But additional ECB
rate hikes could lead to further curve flattening, compressing bank net interest margins
and contributing to slower growth—particularly given that rate increases are coming in
response to supply-driven inflation rather than reflecting stronger economic activity.
We nonetheless continue to see pockets of strength in European markets. Growing
defense budgets should remain a tailwind for Industrials as national governments look to

meet their North Atlantic Treaty Organization targets over the next several years. And the
sector should be further supported by indirect exposure to AI investment through electric
cabling, power infrastructure and building materials for data centers. Leaders in renewable
energy equipment and services are also likely to be beneficiaries of grid upgrades and
broadening electrification globally, particularly given elevated fossil fuel prices. But our view

on the region remains cautious overall. On top of growing uncertainty from the upcoming
electoral cycle and interest rate outlook, rising competition and slowing demand from
China continue to challenge key segments of Europe’s Consumer Discretionary sector.
Limited direct AI concentration in technology hardware, cloud services and application
software also leaves Europe underexposed to the key growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and emerging Asia.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Cautious Optimism Entering Fiscal Year 2027
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
“A renewed focus on rebuilding fiscal space is urgent, given elevated debt, higher
borrowing costs, multiplying demands on public budgets and heightened uncertainty.” So
warned the IMF about the global fiscal outlook last week in its updated World Economic

Outlook report. Much is at stake for developed nations in Europe and Asia facing high
existing debt, unfavorable demographics and more modest growth.
For the U.S., between structurally higher mandatory outlays, the administration’s $1.5T
proposed defense package, and the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield sitting above 5%, spending
is likely headed higher. We continue to fade concerns of an impending U.S. debt crisis,
however, largely because economic growth continues to surprise to the upside. Expanding

output is good news for the debt-to-GDP ratio, which remains a touch below 100%.
Indeed, rather than being led higher on account of un-anchored inflation expectations, the
10-year U.S. Treasury yield has repriced to reflect better-than-expected nominal growth
(Exhibit 5A). Higher yields in the face of slowing growth, meanwhile, would be a different
story, not only for the fiscal outlook (worse) but also for U.S. equity valuations (lower).
Of course, after the U.S. accumulated another $2T deficit in FY 2026, 2 “rebuilding fiscal

space” in the IMF’s words will be politically unpopular and mathematically challenging.
In our view, the biggest near-term tail risk would be a surprise move lower in growth as
budget deficits at 6% of GDP in good times leave little room for bad times. This isn’t our
base case given robust consumer spending per our internal credit and debit card data and
ongoing investment in the AI buildout and beyond. We expect these two pillars to sustain
growth in 2027.

The other good news is that, contrary to concerns, demand for Treasurys remains robust.
Yields are attractive—that was the message from October’s successful $39B 10-year
Treasury note auction. At the same time, U.S. debt boasts an array of buyers: domestic
households, the Federal Reserve, mutual funds and exchange-traded funds, money market
funds, pension funds, foreign central banks, private investors overseas, and more. Per
Exhibit 5B, whereas foreign official investors once dominated some 40% of the U.S.

Treasury market during the 2008/2009 Great Financial Crisis, their share has fallen to
12% while domestic private investors now account for a majority of holdings. Translated:
demand is more diverse than most appreciate.
Exhibit 5: Higher Yields Meet Shifting Composition of U.S. Treasury Demand.

HOUSEHOLD SPENDING
INDICATOR BULLETIN
Spending resilient despite headwinds
4 November 2026
Key points
• Household spending rose 0.6%mth in
September, returning to growth after
August’s brief decline. Q3 spending increased
2.1%qtr, while volumes rose a more subdued

0.7%qtr, pointing to a sharp acceleration
in price growth through the quarter.
• Services spending rebounded and goods
spending remained supportive. Non-
discretionary spending outperformed
discretionary categories, with higher fuel
spending providing significant support.
• In real terms, spending growth over the
quarter was led by transport but with
most other categories also recording
gains. All states except Tasmania
recorded positive volume growth

in Q3, led by WA and Victoria.
• Today’s volumes outcome was broadly in
line with expectations and consistent with
our consumption outlook ahead of the Q3
National Accounts (due 2 December).

### Page 2

are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The monthly household spending indicator rose 0.6%mth in
September (nominal), exceeding both Westpac and market
expectations. We had anticipated a 0.5%mth contraction
(on the basis of a softer read on vehicle sales in the month),

while the market had pencilled in a 0.2%mth rise. Today’s
result instead marks a return to growth following August’s
brief contraction (revised down 0.1ppt to –0.1%mth), with
annual growth strengthening to 6.8%yr. For Q3 as a whole,
nominal spending rose 2.1%qtr, the strongest quarterly
result since the December quarter of 2022. In real terms,
volumes increased a more subdued 0.7%qtr, pointing to a
sharp acceleration in price growth through the quarter.

Non-discretionary spending outperforms
By broad category, services spending more than reversed
last month’s decline, rising a solid 1.1%mth (+6.8%yr). Goods
spending held around its recent pace, increasing 0.3%mth
(+6.7%yr). Discretionary spending returned to growth in
September, rising 0.3%mth (+6.9%yr), although this failed to
fully unwind August’s decline.
The relative strength in non-discretionary spending observed
last month continued into September, with the category rising

1.2%mth (+6.5%yr). While the headline result was stronger,
the breakdown aligns with findings from our Westpac–DataX
Card Tracker, and Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel  which
show discretionary goods continuing to underperform other
categories (see here and here).
At the detailed level, transport and health led the gains,
both rising 1.5%mth. The increase in transport spending was
largely attributable to higher fuel spending, up 5.4%mth,
reflecting elevated fuel prices, with volumes down 2.2%mth.

The ABS also noted higher spending on vehicle repairs
and maintenance, while domestic air travel increased as
households travelled to attend major sporting events.
Recreation & culture rose 0.8%mth, while miscellaneous
goods & services (+0.6%mth), hotels, cafes & restaurants
(+0.5%mth), and clothing & footwear (+0.1%mth) also
recorded gains.
Partially offsetting these increases, food spending declined
0.1%mth, while furnishings & household equipment fell

0.2%mth, likely reflecting the ongoing weakness in housing
turnover (see here). Alcoholic beverages & tobacco recorded
the largest decline, falling 0.7%mth.
State outcomes were positive across the board in September.
WA led the gains, rising 1.1%mth and reversing last
month’s decline. Victoria also rebounded (+1.0%mth),
while Queensland (+0.6%mth), SA (+0.6%mth), Tasmania
(+0.5%mth) and NSW (+0.3%mth) all recorded increases.
Spending returns to growth in September

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts 
are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Volume growth remains firm 
Nominal spending rose a firm 2.1%qtr in Q3, while volumes 
increased a more subdued 0.7%qtr, matching the pace recorded 
in Q2. This implies a sharp acceleration in price growth to 
1.4%qtr, from 0.6%qtr in Q2, and marks the strongest quarterly 
increase in prices since the December quarter of 2022. Through 
the year, volume growth strengthened to 3.2%yr, remaining 
well above the 1.8%yr average recorded through 2025.
Transport led gains in volume terms, rising 1.6%qtr, 
likely partly reflecting base effects in air travel following 
disruptions associated with the Middle East conflict in the June 
quarter. Clothing & footwear (+1.2%qtr), health (+1.1%qtr), 
miscellaneous goods & services (+1.1%qtr), food (+0.5%qtr), 
recreation & culture (+0.5%qtr), and furnishings & household 
equipment (+0.1%qtr) also recorded increases. Hotels, cafes 
& restaurants spending declined 0.1%qtr in volume terms, 
while alcoholic beverages & tobacco was the main drag, falling 
1.2%qtr.
Most states recorded real spending growth over the quarter, 
led by WA (+1.4%qtr), Victoria (+0.9%qtr), NSW (+0.5%qtr), 
Queensland (+0.5%qtr) and SA (+0.4%qtr). Tasmania lagged its 
state peers, recording a 0.1%qtr decline.
Conclusion
The household spending indicator provides a useful guide to 
around two-thirds of total consumer expenditure. Importantly, 
it excludes several major categories included in the National 
Accounts measure of real household consumption, including 
rents and utilities.

Today’s volumes outcome was broadly in line with our 
expectations, with Westpac anticipating around a 0.6%qtr rise 
in Q3, and remains consistent with our view on consumption 
ahead of the Q3 National Accounts (due 2 December). 
However, this will likely be the high water mark for spending. 
The renewed deterioration in global energy markets and the 
cumulative effect of four rate increases over the first three 
quarters of the year are likely to weigh more heaveily on 
household spending in Q4.

Looking further ahead, households will continue to face 
a restrictive monetary policy environment, with the RBA 
delivering an additional rate increase at yesterday’s November 
meeting (see here). Combined with persistent consumer 
pessimism, ongoing cost-of-living pressures (see here) and a 
housing downturn impacting household wealth, spending looks 
set to slow further in 2027.

Document 400: NZ Q3 Labour market data - Potential RBNZ implications (2026-11-09)

Source PDF: all report pdf/NZ Q3 Labour market data - Potential RBNZ implications (2026-11-09).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-09
Pages: 11
Status: ok

### Page 1

#labour #newzealand
NZ Q3 Labour market data - Potential RBNZ implications
By Kelly Eckhold
Chief Economist, Westpac New Zealand
13:16 November 04 2026 Share
The Q3 labour market statistics were weaker than expected. We discuss potential
implications for the RBNZ noting that a December hike remains more likely than not at this
point.
Q3 Labour market data: potential RBNZ implications
The Q3 labour market data were weaker than RBNZ and our expectations.

### Page 2

Today's labour market data seem unequivocally weaker than expected by either us or the RBNZ. While
likely not a gamechanger for the cycle overall, it will add ammunition to the arguments of the MPC
doves that a pause in interest rate rises will make sense soon - perhaps in December or in the New
Year. It’s likely the case that a bit much was priced into December, so the change in market
probabilities in the wake of today’s data makes sense - although my base case remains that a
December rate rise remains more likely than not.
The message from the RBNZ in the October meeting though was to be careful about pricing in "done
deals" and that the outlook will be data-dependent. As we noted at the time, alongside measures of
inflation expectations, the key domestic data to watch ahead of the RBNZ’s 9 December meeting will
be indicators of what is happening to excess capacity (the output gap) as this will be a key determinant
of the Bank’s assessment of the outlook for inflation over the coming year.
It’s useful to remember in the RBNZ’s forecasting framework that GDP figures are the main
determinant of excess capacity in the economy (i.e., the output gap). The Q2 GDP data told us that the
output gap probably narrowed a bit through Q2, mainly reflecting historical revisions. In light of today’s
labour market numbers, the forecast for growth in Q3 may have some downside risk - but there are
conflicting indicators there, so it’s not that clear. However, it’s probably hard to argue that the level of
excess capacity reduced further in Q3 given today’s data. 
In the eyes of dovish MPC members, the rise in the unemployment rate and other measures important
for the RBNZ's labour market dashboard likely push in the direction of having greater comfort with the
medium-term inflation outlook. The vacancy to unemployment ratio increased a bit, labour constraints
eased a bit, and the unemployment rate has increased. Hence from a labour market perspective it
could be argued that policy could have more time all else equal. The outcome of the election, with the
possibility of a new RBNZ mandate, could increase the prominence of these factors - although this
won’t likely be an explicit factor in the RBNZ’s thinking in December even though it may be in the minds
of individual MPC members.
It could be the case that the argument for increases in the OCR track through 2027 could be
undermined with this data. It’s important to note (probably self-evidently) that there are other data that
will also be relevant. Hence the risk of a pause earlier in the New Year has probably increased with this
data, all else equal. I don't think our forecast peak OCR is really at issue here, but the time taken to get
there could be more flexible. Takes along the lines of "3% is the terminal rate" are very premature and
misplaced, in our view.

A December hike remains more likely than not, but there will be a clear debate to be had between the
MPC hawks and doves.
The forecast peak OCR is likely less impacted than the time to reach that peak. Further pauses in the
tightening cycle could come earlier than currently forecast. 
The outlook for the exchange rate is especially clouded by this data should it take a longer time for
NZ rates to move closer to global norms. 
Watch for updated RBNZ commentary on 10 and 19 November. The Governor’s observation that it
would have been useful to update markets ahead of the October meeting could imply a greater
willingness to comment in the coming weeks.

### Page 3

The outlook for the exchange rate is undermined by this data. I feel very happy about our year-end
target of 79c for the NZD/AUD rate with the risk that a recovery in the NZD takes longer than currently
assumed.
Looking ahead, there are some opportunities for the RBNZ to update markets on their views. Top of
mind is Paul Conway's 10 November speech to the UBS Australasia Conference. The Governor is also
speaking to the Institute of Directors on 19 November. If the RBNZ has something to say, then it might
happen in conjunction with these events (the 10 November speech is titled “How is the NZ economy
tracking”, so it will be difficult for Conway to avoid saying something about how he is interpreting
today’s data). We also note the Governor's comments in the October MPR that it could have been
useful to have been more flexible about updating markets on emerging views of new economic data.
This might increase the likelihood that these events could be used to provide guidance on the RBNZ’s
views of this data (and other emerging events), especially if it feels that the market’s interpretation is at
odds with its own.

Document 415: Q3 Growth in advanced economies_ solid despite shocks (2026-10-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Q3 Growth in advanced economies_ solid despite shocks (2026-10-23).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 23 October 2026

EcoFlash

Q3 Growth in advanced economies: solid despite shocks

Growth looks to have held up in Q3 despite successive shocks (see our analysis), as it did in 2025 and H1 2026. A longer 
Middle East conflict has fed into more persistent inflation; drought has worsened in many countries, and interest rates 
have risen further – at the short end, with both the ECB and the Fed raising policy rates in September, and even more at 
the long end. However, our nowcasts show that growth is bending without breaking in most advanced economies. GDP 
figures (due on 29 October in the US and 30 October for the euro area and its member states) are expected to confirm 
that. In the Euro area, momentum looks stable (excluding Ireland, whose GDP is volatile). In Germany, growth has been 
uninterrupted since Q4 2025, while France is likely to have returned to growth after stagnating in H1. In the US, growth 
is likely to have gathered pace, according to the GDP Now, still driven by AI, but increasingly by other sectors as well.

Euro area: resilience beneath the surface. Our nowcast points to growth slowing to 0.3% in q/q, though the pace is stable 
excluding Ireland (Irish GDP is volatile and had markedly boosted the Q2 figure). Excluding Ireland, growth should hold at its H2 
average, a sign of the bloc’s underlying resilience. Also, momentum has broadened to a growing number of sectors ( see our 
analysis). While industrial production stalled over the summer, services remained buoyant. The services PMI rebounded sharply 
in Q3 and sits firmly in expansion territory (53.0 in September). Improving job-related survey data (PMI and European Commission 
surveys) and a stable unemployment rate (6.4% in August) hint at more hiring in Q3 – a support for household consumption that 
partly offsets purchasing-power losses from persistent inflation. Corporate investment is underpinned by buoyant new investment 
lending (8.7% y/y In August, 12-month cumulative). 
France: growing, nevertheless. The succession of shocks does not appear to have prevented France from returning to 
growth in Q3, at 0.2% q/q, according to our nowcast. One-off factors accounted for the stall in activity in H1. In Q3, the drought 
worsened and industrial production remained subdued. However, market services production rebounded in July and August, 
notably in business services, restaurants, digital and real estate. The latter points to residential investment recovering after a weak 
H1. Corporate investment likely followed, as reflected in buoyant credit flows and AI-related investment. Household consumption 
is likely to have improved slightly on higher electric vehicle sales.  
Euro area  France

Source: Refinitiv, BNP Paribas  Source: Refinitiv, BNP Paribas

Germany: growth still solid. The H1 rebound in activity is moderating but continues. Our nowcast points to a fourth straight 
quarter of growth in Q3, at 0.2% q/q (after 0.3% in Q4 2025, 0.4% in Q1 2026, and 0.3% in Q2). Manufacturing activity eased 
slightly, according to the September PMI survey (53.9, down 0.4 m/m). Government spending will remain growth-supportive, with 
defence and infrastructure outlays gaining traction. Infrastructure spending continues to underpin activity in public works, which 
stands at its highest since November 2021 (2.5% 3m/3m in August). Exports (4.4% y/y between January and August) remain the 
main growth engine, helped by a pivot towards Europe (7.2%). 
United States: an expansion undeterred by inflation. Growth is likely to have gathered pace in Q3. The Atlanta Fed’s GDP 
Now points to a 3.5% annualized growth rate (AR), close to our own 3.7% forecast (0.9% non -AR) and up from 2.2% in 
Q2. Domestic demand remains the engine. Consumption is weathering the pickup in inflation, while the AI-driven capex cycle 
remains robust – as reflected in retail sales and nondefence capital-goods orders (excl. aircraft). As a result, growth in final sales

### Page 2

to private domestic purchasers 1 should print above 4% AR again. By contrast, external trade should remain a drag on growth, 
reflecting AI-related import needs. Survey data point to mome ntum that is both solid and broad -based. The ISM indices have 
remained firmly in growth territory (54.9 quarterly average for manufacturing and services alike). At the same time, they highlight 
persistent price and supply-chain pressures.

Germany   United States

Source: Refinitiv, BNP Paribas   Source: Refinitiv, Atlanta Fed, BNP Paribas

2026 年 11 月 4 日
